- **zimmer-change-ai-artifact**: Add or change an AI artifact in this repo — a skill, agent root, MCP server, plugin, hook, or reference. Zimmer ships a SELF-CONTAINED AIR catalog: the top-level artifact indexes wired by `air.json` are the catalog, resolved offline with no network and no private GitHub catalogs. Covers where each artifact type lives, how `default_in_roots` makes an artifact default-on, the resolution invariants the whole test suite depends on, and how to verify a change before pushing. An artifact change is NOT an app bug — do not go looking for one in `app/`. ([tadasant/zimmer](https://github.com/tadasant/zimmer/tree/HEAD/skills/zimmer-change-ai-artifact) · ★1)
- **zimmer-debug-staging**: Diagnose a broken or silent Zimmer staging deployment. Covers the four places staging's truth lives (the deploy run, the containers on the droplet, the OTLP logs in VictoriaLogs, the errors in GlitchTip), the `bin/rails obs:status` / `obs:smoke` diagnostics that exist because Zimmer's telemetry is a HARD NO-OP when misconfigured, LogsQL queries scoped to `deployment.environment=staging`, and the specific failure modes that make staging look healthy while doing nothing. Use when a staging deploy fails, when sessions hang, or when telemetry is missing. ([tadasant/zimmer](https://github.com/tadasant/zimmer/tree/HEAD/skills/zimmer-debug-staging) · ★1)
- **zimmer-deploy-staging**: Deploy a Zimmer change to the staging environment. Staging is a PERSISTENT Kamal-deployed droplet, and the `Deploy staging` workflow is manual-dispatch only — it takes a `ref` input, so it can build and deploy an UNMERGED feature branch, which is the main reason to use it. Covers dispatching the run, the Kamal container swap, watching the deploy, rolling back, and what an agent session can and cannot do without DO/Tailscale credentials. There is NO production deploy workflow in this repo. ([tadasant/zimmer](https://github.com/tadasant/zimmer/tree/HEAD/skills/zimmer-deploy-staging) · ★1)
- **zimmer-run-tests**: Run the right slice of Zimmer's test suite for a change and reproduce CI locally. Covers the four tiers (Minitest unit/integration, Capybara system tests, Playwright e2e scripts, the standalone GHCR retention test), which of them CI actually gates on, the Postgres + Redis prerequisites, and the lint / Brakeman / Gemfile.lock jobs that fail PRs as often as the tests do. Use before pushing any change to this repo. ([tadasant/zimmer](https://github.com/tadasant/zimmer/tree/HEAD/skills/zimmer-run-tests) · ★1)
- **zimmer-start-dev-server**: Bring up a local Zimmer instance (Rails 8 + Tailwind watcher via foreman) to develop against, exercise a change by hand, or take screenshots. Covers the Postgres + Redis prerequisites the app does NOT provision for you, the `bin/dev` vs `bin/setup` split, port handling, and the failure modes that look like a broken app but are really a missing service. Use before any change that needs to be verified in a running app rather than only in tests. ([tadasant/zimmer](https://github.com/tadasant/zimmer/tree/HEAD/skills/zimmer-start-dev-server) · ★1)

- **zinb-autoencoder-denoising**: Use when a raw scRNA-seq count matrix (e.g. gene x cell CSV/TSV or AnnData h5ad) is sparse with dropouts and you need a denoised/imputed matrix that preserves count structure for downstream visualization or clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-autoencoder-denoising--730aaee349617cbf))
- **zinb-autoencoder-denoising**: Use when pre-training a source classification model on labeled scRNA-seq count data and a denoising module that models dropout and count dispersion (ZINB NLL) is required before source-free adaptation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-autoencoder-denoising--959defd32306be5e))
- **zinb-autoencoder-dropout-estimation**: Use when scRNA-seq expression data contain dropout events that need imputation prior to downstream clustering or gene-expression analysis, and a distribution-aware (ZINB prior) reconstruction is preferred over simple smoothing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-autoencoder-dropout-estimation))
- **zinb-autoencoder-loss-composition**: Use when clustering scRNA-seq count data with partial pairwise prior knowledge (must-link/cannot-link pairs) that should softly shape the latent embedding instead of hard-constrained clustering like COP K-means. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-autoencoder-loss-composition))
- **zinb-autoencoder-variant-configuration**: Use when dissecting which scGCL component (ZINB encoder, graph convolution, AFGRL contrastive learning) drives clustering/imputation results, i.e. an internal component-ablation on Adam/Romanov h5 scRNA-seq data rather than a cross-method benchmark. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-autoencoder-variant-configuration))
- **zinb-decoder-autoencoder-training**: Use when benchmarking decoder choices for scRNA-seq clustering, e.g. reproducing the scMGCA ablation that swaps the multinomial decoder for a ZINB likelihood on count matrices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-decoder-autoencoder-training))
- **zinb-differential-chromatin-interaction-testing**: Use when you have a normalized scHi-C count matrix (rows = bin pairs, columns = samples/cells) plus a two-condition factor vector and need single-cell DCI calls that handle excessive zero counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-differential-chromatin-interaction-testing))
- **zinb-gaussian-decoder-modeling**: Use when integrating matched spot-level ST count matrices and continuous SM metabolite intensity matrices from one or more samples, and you need modality-faithful reconstruction plus batch correction in a shared latent embedding. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/zinb-gaussian-decoder-modeling))
- **zinb-gene-expression-likelihood-modeling**: Use when building or reimplementing a VAE-based single-cell integration model (e.g., scDREAMER) where the decoder must reconstruct raw UMI counts with a ZINB likelihood and KL-regularized latent terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-gene-expression-likelihood-modeling))
- **zinb-low-dimensional-signal-extraction**: Use when raw scRNA-seq counts need dimensionality reduction that handles zero inflation without prior normalization, or when unwanted variation (e.g., batch, detection rate) must be removed while preserving biological structure such as patient or cell-type effects. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-low-dimensional-signal-extraction))
- **zinb-model-estimation-single-cell**: Use when you have a raw scRNA-seq count matrix (cells x genes) and need a low-dimensional embedding that models zero inflation and over-dispersion, e.g. for clustering, t-SNE input, or batch-effect removal. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-model-estimation-single-cell))
- **zinb-model-fitting-count-data**: Use when raw scRNA-seq count data show zero inflation (dropouts) and over-dispersion, and you need model-based estimates of mean counts, zero probabilities, and dispersion, or a goodness-of-fit comparison of ZINB vs NB/hurdle count models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-model-fitting-count-data))
- **zinb-model-fitting-scrnaseq**: Use when raw scRNA-seq UMI count matrices need dimensionality reduction that models zero inflation and over-dispersion directly, replacing PCA or ZIFA without a preliminary normalization step. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-model-fitting-scrnaseq))
- **zinb-noise-model-parameterization**: Use when denoising or imputing sparse, overdispersed scRNA-seq count matrices where dropout zero counts must be modeled explicitly and classical imputation with defined missing values is unsuitable. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-noise-model-parameterization))
- **zinb-reconstruction-loss-implementation**: Use when reconstructing, retraining, or verifying Pollock's VAE in PyTorch: the decoder must reproduce counts via a ZINB loss weighted by --zinb-scaler, alongside KL and classification terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-reconstruction-loss-implementation))
- **zinb-reconstruction-loss-modeling**: Use when training a deep autoencoder (e.g. scMDC-style multimodal denoising autoencoder) on raw single-cell count data whose mRNA/ADT/ATAC matrices are zero-inflated and over-dispersed, and reconstruction must model dropout rather than use a plain Gaussian/MSE loss. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-reconstruction-loss-modeling))
- **zinb-scrna-seq-data-reconstruction**: Use when you have bulk RNA-seq data for cohort samples plus a single-cell reference (e.g., sequenced representative AP6) and need inferred single-cell profiles (.h5ad) for target samples at true single-cell resolution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-scrna-seq-data-reconstruction))
- **zinb-to-nb-loss-replacement-in-autoencoder**: Use when you need to run or reproduce a ZINB-vs-NB loss ablation in a scRNA-seq deep clustering model, i.e. when the analysis goal is to determine whether explicit zero-inflation modeling changes clustering performance (NMI/ARI). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-to-nb-loss-replacement-in-autoencoder))
- **zinb-wave-latent-factor-extraction**: Use when raw scRNA-seq count matrices (e.g., GEO GSE95601 olfactory epithelium data) need a low-dimensional representation that handles zero inflation and over-dispersion without prior normalization, especially as input to clustering or Slingshot lineage inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-wave-latent-factor-extraction))
- **zinb-wave-signal-extraction-on-raw-counts**: Use when scRNA-seq raw count matrices need low-dimensional signal extraction or dimensionality reduction, and preliminary normalization (TC, TMM, FQ) is undesirable or unknown. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/zinb-wave-signal-extraction-on-raw-counts))
- **zinbave-zero-inflation-weighting**: Use when preparing sparse single-cell isoform count matrices — especially after balancing/downsampling cell numbers across groups — for differential isoform usage (DIU) testing with DESeq2 or edgeR via acorde. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/zinbave-zero-inflation-weighting))

- **zitima-grok**: Εκτέλεσε ζήτημα (GitHub issue) που άνοιξε bot της ομάδας Grok και πήρε την ετικέτα claude. Χρησιμοποίησέ το όταν η εργασία προέρχεται από ζήτημα με ετικέτα grok ή claude, όταν το κείμενο μιας εργασίας το έγραψε άλλο εργαλείο, ή όταν ζητείται «κάνε ό,τι λέει το ζήτημα». Ορίζει τι ελέγχεται πριν αγγίξεις κώδικα και ποιες διαδρομές δεν αλλάζουν ποτέ αυτόματα.
